DESIGN.md

show
## Concept

The player taps to instantly warp the dot forward a set distance, leaping over upcoming obstacles. The warp has a short cooldown, so timing each jump is critical and satisfying.

## Player

The avatar is a glowing quantum dot that moves continuously forward through a scrolling tunnel. Its only verb is "warp" (triggered by a tap/click) that instantly displaces it forward by a fixed amount; after warping it enters a brief cooldown during which no further warps can be performed.

## Core loop

1. The dot drifts forward at a steady pace while obstacles scroll toward it.
2. The player watches the next obstacles and decides whether to warp now or wait.
3. When they tap, the dot instantly leaps forward, bypassing any obstacles within the warp range, then enters the cooldown timer.
4. The cycle repeats, with obstacles becoming denser as the game progresses, forcing the player to balance risk and timing.

## Hazards / Obstacles

- **Static blocks**: Fixed-size barriers that occupy part of the tunnel; a warp must clear them completely.
- **Sweeping barriers**: Horizontal or vertical sweeps that move across the path; timing a warp to avoid their active phase is required.
- **Pulsing spikes**: Spikes that appear and disappear in sync with a rhythm; warping during the "off" phase lets the player pass.
- **Projectile bursts**: Short-lived bursts that travel forward; if the player is within their path they die; a correctly timed warp can outrun them.

## Score

- Each successful warp that clears at least one obstacle awards points.
- Each obstacle passed without warping (by simply drifting) gives a small bonus.
- Combo multiplier increases when multiple warps are performed in quick succession.

## Win and lose

- Lose: Collision with any obstacle while not in a warp state ends the game instantly.
- Win: Reaching a predefined distance (e.g., the end of the level) or surviving a set time threshold triggers a victory screen.

## Feel

The game feels tense yet rhythmic, with a steady forward motion punctuated by crisp, instant jumps. Visuals are neon-glow on a dark backdrop, evoking a futuristic tunnel. Audio consists of a sharp "whoosh" on each warp and subtle ambient pulses that heighten anticipation.

## Why it's fun

The thrill of pulling off a perfectly-timed warp just before an obstacle hits creates that instant "yes!" moment that makes players grin or gasp with delight.

TASKS.md

show
1. HTML Scaffold + meta.json — create index.html with header, canvas, restart button and empty script, and matching meta.json file following CONVENTIONS.md.
2. Game State Objects — define player, hazards, score, and phase objects with initial values at top of script.
3. Main Loop — implement requestAnimationFrame loop with startLoop/stop functionality, clearing canvas each frame.
4. Input System — set up keys Set with keydown/keyup listeners, R-key restart, and restart button click hook to reset().
5. Player Movement — implement dt‑based physics using inputs, enforce max speed and friction, ensure controls affect player state.
6. Hazard System — define hazard data structure, spawn logic with SAFE_RADIUS at start, dt‑scaled motion.
7. Collision Detection — detect player‑hazard collisions, switch phase to gameover and stop gameplay.
8. Scoring — increase score on discrete events, draw score on canvas top‑left each frame.
9. Rendering — draw background, hazards, player (high contrast), HUD; ensure canvas clears each frame.
10. Game‑Over Overlay — dim background, show “Game Over”, final score, and restart instructions.
11. Observable Contract — populate window.__game with state, score, player position/visibility, and objective each frame.
12. Audio — lazy‑init AudioContext, beep() helper, hook sound effects to hit, score, death events.
13. Polish & Edge Cases — ensure restart fully cancels prior loop, enforce dt clamping, handle loop errors, verify meta.json matches header.
14. Final Validation — run a quick sanity check: page loads without errors, visible canvas content, controls work, observable contract present.

<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Quantum Leap</h1>
    <p class="game-description">Tap to instantly warp the quantum dot forward, leaping over obstacles. Time your jumps and survive as long as possible.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Tap or click to warp</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
    // === CONFIG =============================================================


    // === STATE ==============================================================
    const canvas = document.getElementById('game');
    const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d');
    const restartBtn = document.getElementById('restart');

    let player = { x: canvas.width / 2, y: canvas.height - 30, radius: 8 };
    let hazards = []; // array of hazard objects
    let stars = []; // background star field
    let score = 0;
    let phase = "menu"; // "menu" | "playing" | "gameover"
    let keys = new Set();
    let lastTime = performance.now();
    let loopId = null;
    let audioCtx = null;

    // === RESET ==============================================================
    function reset() {
      // TODO: implement reset logic later
    }

    // === INPUT ==============================================================
    document.addEventListener('keydown', (ev) => {
      if (ev.key === 'r' || ev.key === 'R') { reset(); return; }
      keys.add(ev.key);
    });
    document.addEventListener('keyup', (ev) => { keys.delete(ev.key); });
    restartBtn.addEventListener('click', reset);
    // === UPDATE ==============================================================
    function update(dt) {
      // Placeholder: increase score over time
      score += Math.floor(dt * 10);
    }

    // === RENDER ==============================================================
    function render() {
      // Clear canvas
      ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

      // Draw hazards (if any)
      for (const h of hazards) {
        // Expect hazard object: {x, y, w, h, color, alive}
        if (h.alive === false) continue;
        ctx.fillStyle = h.color || "#ff5252"; // default color
        ctx.fillRect(h.x, h.y, h.w, h.h);
      }

      // Draw player
      ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, player.radius, 0, Math.PI * 2);
      ctx.fill();

      // Draw HUD (score)
      ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
      ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.textAlign = "left";
      ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);

      // Game‑over overlay
      if (phase === "gameover") {
        ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(10,10,15,0.7)";
        ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
        ctx.textAlign = "center";
        ctx.font = "44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
        ctx.fillStyle = "#ff5252";
        ctx.fillText("Game Over", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 - 20);
        ctx.font = "20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
        ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
        ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}`, canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 14);
        ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
        ctx.fillStyle = "#a5a5b8";
        ctx.fillText("Press R or click Restart", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 42);
      }
    }

    // === LOOP ==============================================================
    function startLoop() {
      if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
      function tick(now) {
        try {
          const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
          lastTime = now;
          update(dt);
          render();
        } catch (err) {
          console.error("loop error", err);
        }
        loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }

    // Boot the game
    reset();
    startLoop();
  </script>
</body>
</html>

Never make the player wait.\n92: 2. **Clear feedback** — the player always knows when something happened. Use color flashes, particles, brief screen shake, score popups, or sound on every meaningful event (hit, score, miss, death).\n93: 3. **Win/lose state** — the game has a clear end condition. On lose, draw \"Game Over\" + final score + restart instructions on the canvas. On win, celebrate (color burst, \"You Win!\" overlay).\n94: 4. **Restart without page reload** — both pressing R AND clicking the `<button id=\"restart\">` reset the game cleanly via the same code path. Mention both in `controls` (e.g. \"Press R or click Restart\"). On restart you MUST `cancelAnimationFrame` any pending loop before starting a new one (otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game eventually slows to a crawl).\n95: 5. **Progressive difficulty** — start gentle so the player can learn, then ramp up. Don't dump them into chaos at t=0.\n96: 6. **No dead time** — something interesting on screen at all times. Idle background animation (stars, gradient pulse, particles) is fine.\n97: 7. **Persistent score / progress UI** — drawn on the canvas at the top corner, always visible.\n98: 8. **Audio (optional but encouraged)** — generate tones with `OscillatorNode` (no external assets). **Do not auto-start audio on page load** — browsers block this and it will throw. Initialize the `AudioContext` lazily on the first keypress or click. Wrap audio code in try/catch.\n99: \n100: ## Playable Contract — non-negotiable\n101: \n102: These are not aspirations. A game that violates any of these is broken even if it runs without errors. After your implementation finishes, the validator loads the page in a real browser and runs an 8-second playtest with synthetic input — failures here are reported back to you and must be fixed before the game ships.\n103: \n104: ### 1. The player can lose\n105: \n106: There must be a collision check (or other end condition) that fires `gameOver` and stops the active gameplay loop. \"Game runs forever, no death\" is broken. Examples that have failed in the past: collision check compared a value against itself; \"spike\" collision only clamped position instead of killing the player; obstacles never reached the player.\n107: \n108: ### 2. The player can score\n109: \n110: The score must increase on a **discrete event** (a survived obstacle, a collected pickup, an enemy destroyed, a beat hit). \"Score = elapsed seconds\" is allowed but weak. Score must be drawn on the canvas at all times.\n111: \n112: ### 3. The first 3 seconds are survivable with no input\n113: \n114: Define a `SAFE_RADIUS` (e.g. 200px) around the player's starting position. When spawning hazards, **reject any spawn that is within `SAFE_RADIUS` of the player AND would reach them within 3 seconds at current hazard speed**. Pre-spawning hazards on top of the player at t=0 has caused frame-1 deaths in past games. Don't do it.\n115: \n116: ### 4. The control actually changes the outcome\n117: \n118: Whatever the player presses must, in the next frame, change a value that affects collision or score. If a control is purely cosmetic (e.g. \"stretch horizontally\" but the gaps are vertical), the game is fundamentally broken even if it runs. After writing, **re-read the game's concept and trace one frame**: does the input do what the concept claims?\n119: \n120: ### 5. dt-based physics, not per-frame constants\n121: \n122: All movement and timing must scale by delta time (seconds since last frame). Per-frame constants (`x += 5` every frame) make games run 2.4× faster on a 144Hz display.\n123: \n124: ```js\n125: let lastTime = performance.now();\n126: function loop(now) {\n127:   const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1); // seconds, clamped\n128:   lastTime = now;\n129:   // physics scaled by dt:\n130:   player.x += player.vx * dt;\n131:   enemy.y  += enemy.vy  * dt;\n132:   spawnTimer -= dt;\n133:   // ...\n134:   requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n135: }\n136: requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n137: ```\n138: \n139: ### 6. That function MUST `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` the previous loop before scheduling a new one — otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game slows to a crawl over time.\n142: \n143: ```js\n144: let loopId = null;\n145: function startLoop() {\n146:   if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);\n147:   function tick(now) { /* ... */ loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick); }\n148:   loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n149: }\n150: ```\n151: \n152: ### 7. ## Concept

The player taps to instantly warp the dot forward a set distance, leaping over upcoming obstacles. The warp has a short cooldown, so timing each jump is critical and satisfying.

## Player

The avatar is a glowing quantum dot that moves continuously forward through a scrolling tunnel. Its only verb is "warp" (triggered by a tap/click) that instantly displaces it forward by a fixed amount; after warping it enters a brief cooldown during which no further warps can be performed.

## Core loop

1. The dot drifts forward at a steady pace while obstacles scroll toward it.
2. The player watches the next obstacles and decides whether to warp now or wait.
3. When they tap, the dot instantly leaps forward, bypassing any obstacles within the warp range, then enters the cooldown timer.
4. The cycle repeats, with obstacles becoming denser as the game progresses, forcing the player to balance risk and timing.

## Hazards / Obstacles

- **Static blocks**: Fixed-size barriers that occupy part of the tunnel; a warp must clear them completely.
- **Sweeping barriers**: Horizontal or vertical sweeps that move across the path; timing a warp to avoid their active phase is required.
- **Pulsing spikes**: Spikes that appear and disappear in sync with a rhythm; warping during the "off" phase lets the player pass.
- **Projectile bursts**: Short-lived bursts that travel forward; if the player is within their path they die; a correctly timed warp can outrun them.

## Score

- Each successful warp that clears at least one obstacle awards points.
- Each obstacle passed without warping (by simply drifting) gives a small bonus.
- Combo multiplier increases when multiple warps are performed in quick succession.

## Win and lose

- Lose: Collision with any obstacle while not in a warp state ends the game instantly.
- Win: Reaching a predefined distance (e.g., the end of the level) or surviving a set time threshold triggers a victory screen.

## Feel

The game feels tense yet rhythmic, with a steady forward motion punctuated by crisp, instant jumps. Visuals are neon-glow on a dark backdrop, evoking a futuristic tunnel. Audio consists of a sharp "whoosh" on each warp and subtle ambient pulses that heighten anticipation.

## Why it's fun

The thrill of pulling off a perfectly-timed warp just before an obstacle hits creates that instant "yes!" moment that makes players grin or gasp with delight.

All ASCII characters only.

prompt (13,835 chars)
## Reference example: "breakout"

The following is a complete, validator-passing game. **Use it as a structural template** — your output must follow the same section ordering, the same patterns for the loop / restart / observable contract, and the same overall code style. Do NOT reproduce this game; you are building a different game described in the prompt below. The example exists only to anchor structure.

Notice in particular:
- The `// === SECTION ===` divider banners and their fixed order: CONFIG → STATE → RESET → LOOP/UPDATE → RENDER → INPUT → AUDIO → OBSERVABLE CONTRACT → BOOT.
- Plain object literals for game state (no `class`).
- A `keys` Set polled each frame instead of mutating game state from key handlers.
- `if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` at the top of `startLoop()`.
- The animation loop body wrapped in `try/catch`.
- `publishState()` called once per frame at the end of the tick.
- `window.__game` populated honestly (player.visible reflects actual viewport position).
- AudioContext initialized lazily inside `beep()`, wrapped in try/catch.

### Example `meta.json`
```json
{
  "title": "Neon Breaker",
  "description": "Bounce the ball with the paddle and clear every brick. One ball, no second chances.",
  "controls": [
    "Arrow keys or A/D to move the paddle",
    "Hold Space to launch faster",
    "Press R or click Restart"
  ],
  "tags": ["arcade", "breakout", "bouncing"]
}
```

### Example `index.html`
```html
<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
  <meta charset="utf-8">
  <title>Neon Breaker</title>
  <style>
    body { margin: 0; background: #0a0a0f; color: #e6e6f0; font-family: ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif; }
    .game-header { padding: 12px 16px; border-bottom: 1px solid #1d1d2a; }
    .game-header h1 { margin: 0; font-size: 18px; color: #7df9ff; }
    .game-header .game-description { margin: 4px 0; color: #a5a5b8; }
    .game-header .game-controls { margin: 4px 0; padding-left: 18px; color: #c8c8d8; }
    main { display: flex; flex-direction: column; align-items: center; padding: 16px; }
    canvas { background: #0f1320; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; }
    .game-restart { margin-top: 10px; padding: 8px 14px; background: #1c2030; color: #7df9ff; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; cursor: pointer; }
    .game-restart:hover { background: #232842; }
  </style>
</head>
<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Neon Breaker</h1>
    <p class="game-description">Bounce the ball with the paddle and clear every brick. One ball, no second chances.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Arrow keys or A/D to move the paddle</li>
      <li>Hold Space to launch faster</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
  // === CONFIG =============================================================
  const PADDLE_W = 110;
  const PADDLE_H = 14;
  const PADDLE_Y = 560;
  const PADDLE_SPEED = 700;       // px/sec

  const BALL_R = 7;
  const BALL_BASE_SPEED = 360;    // px/sec at start
  const BALL_MAX_SPEED  = 720;
  const BALL_SPEEDUP_PER_HIT = 6; // px/sec added per brick

  const BRICK_ROWS = 6;
  const BRICK_COLS = 12;
  const BRICK_W = 60;
  const BRICK_H = 20;
  const BRICK_GAP = 4;
  const BRICK_TOP = 60;
  const BRICK_LEFT = (800 - (BRICK_COLS * (BRICK_W + BRICK_GAP) - BRICK_GAP)) / 2;
  const ROW_COLORS = ["#ff52ae", "#ff8c52", "#ffd23f", "#a3ff52", "#52ffae", "#7df9ff"];

  // === STATE ==============================================================
  const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
  const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
  const restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");

  let paddle;        // { x } center x
  let ball;          // { x, y, vx, vy, speed }
  let bricks;        // array of { x, y, w, h, color, alive }
  let score;
  let bricksLeft;
  let phase;         // "playing" | "won" | "lost"
  let keys;          // pressed key set
  let highScore = 0;
  let lastTime;
  let loopId = null;
  let audioCtx = null;

  function reset() {
    paddle = { x: 400 };
    ball = { x: 400, y: 480, vx: 0, vy: 0, speed: BALL_BASE_SPEED };
    launchBall();
    bricks = [];
    for (let r = 0; r < BRICK_ROWS; r++) {
      for (let c = 0; c < BRICK_COLS; c++) {
        bricks.push({
          x: BRICK_LEFT + c * (BRICK_W + BRICK_GAP),
          y: BRICK_TOP + r * (BRICK_H + BRICK_GAP),
          w: BRICK_W, h: BRICK_H,
          color: ROW_COLORS[r % ROW_COLORS.length],
          alive: true,
        });
      }
    }
    bricksLeft = bricks.length;
    score = 0;
    phase = "playing";
    keys = new Set();
    lastTime = performance.now();
    publishState();
    startLoop();
  }

  function launchBall() {
    // Always launch upward, mild horizontal lean either side.
    const angle = (-Math.PI / 2) + (Math.random() * 0.6 - 0.3);
    ball.vx = Math.cos(angle) * ball.speed;
    ball.vy = Math.sin(angle) * ball.speed;
  }

  function startLoop() {
    if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
    function tick(now) {
      try {
        const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
        lastTime = now;
        update(dt);
        render();
        publishState();
      } catch (err) {
        console.error("loop error", err);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }

  // === UPDATE =============================================================
  function update(dt) {
    if (phase !== "playing") return;

    // Paddle.
    const dirX = (keys.has("ArrowRight") || keys.has("d") || keys.has("D") ? 1 : 0)
               - (keys.has("ArrowLeft")  || keys.has("a") || keys.has("A") ? 1 : 0);
    const speedMul = keys.has(" ") ? 1.6 : 1;
    paddle.x += dirX * PADDLE_SPEED * speedMul * dt;
    paddle.x = clamp(paddle.x, PADDLE_W / 2, canvas.width - PADDLE_W / 2);

    // Ball motion.
    ball.x += ball.vx * dt;
    ball.y += ball.vy * dt;

    // Walls.
    if (ball.x < BALL_R) { ball.x = BALL_R; ball.vx = Math.abs(ball.vx); beep(220, 0.04); }
    if (ball.x > canvas.width - BALL_R) { ball.x = canvas.width - BALL_R; ball.vx = -Math.abs(ball.vx); beep(220, 0.04); }
    if (ball.y < BALL_R) { ball.y = BALL_R; ball.vy = Math.abs(ball.vy); beep(220, 0.04); }

    // Floor — lose.
    if (ball.y > canvas.height + BALL_R) {
      phase = "lost";
      if (score > highScore) highScore = score;
      beep(110, 0.25);
      return;
    }

    // Paddle.
    const px = paddle.x, py = PADDLE_Y;
    if (ball.vy > 0
        && ball.y + BALL_R >= py
        && ball.y - BALL_R <= py + PADDLE_H
        && ball.x >= px - PADDLE_W / 2 - BALL_R
        && ball.x <= px + PADDLE_W / 2 + BALL_R) {
      // Bounce direction depends on where it hit on the paddle.
      const offset = (ball.x - px) / (PADDLE_W / 2); // -1..1
      const angle = (-Math.PI / 2) + offset * 1.0;   // up-ish, leaning side
      ball.vx = Math.cos(angle) * ball.speed;
      ball.vy = Math.sin(angle) * ball.speed;
      ball.y = py - BALL_R - 0.1;
      beep(540, 0.05);
    }

    // Bricks.
    for (const b of bricks) {
      if (!b.alive) continue;
      if (ball.x + BALL_R < b.x || ball.x - BALL_R > b.x + b.w) continue;
      if (ball.y + BALL_R < b.y || ball.y - BALL_R > b.y + b.h) continue;

      b.alive = false;
      bricksLeft--;
      score += 10;
      ball.speed = Math.min(BALL_MAX_SPEED, ball.speed + BALL_SPEEDUP_PER_HIT);

      // Decide which side was hit by comparing penetrations.
      const overlapX = Math.min(ball.x + BALL_R - b.x, b.x + b.w - (ball.x - BALL_R));
      const overlapY = Math.min(ball.y + BALL_R - b.y, b.y + b.h - (ball.y - BALL_R));
      if (overlapX < overlapY) ball.vx = -ball.vx; else ball.vy = -ball.vy;

      // Renormalize to current speed.
      const m = Math.hypot(ball.vx, ball.vy);
      ball.vx = (ball.vx / m) * ball.speed;
      ball.vy = (ball.vy / m) * ball.speed;
      beep(880, 0.05);
      break;
    }

    if (bricksLeft === 0) {
      phase = "won";
      if (score > highScore) highScore = score;
      beep(990, 0.2);
    }
  }

  // === RENDER =============================================================
  function render() {
    ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

    // Bricks.
    for (const b of bricks) {
      if (!b.alive) continue;
      ctx.fillStyle = b.color;
      ctx.fillRect(b.x, b.y, b.w, b.h);
    }

    // Paddle.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
    ctx.fillRect(paddle.x - PADDLE_W / 2, PADDLE_Y, PADDLE_W, PADDLE_H);

    // Ball.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#ffffff";
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.arc(ball.x, ball.y, BALL_R, 0, Math.PI * 2);
    ctx.fill();

    // HUD.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
    ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
    ctx.textAlign = "left";
    ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
    ctx.textAlign = "right";
    ctx.fillText(`Bricks ${bricksLeft}  ·  Best ${highScore}`, canvas.width - 12, 22);

    if (phase === "lost" || phase === "won") {
      ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(10,10,15,0.7)";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
      ctx.textAlign = "center";
      ctx.font = "44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillStyle = phase === "won" ? "#52ffae" : "#ff52ae";
      ctx.fillText(phase === "won" ? "You Win!" : "Game Over", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 - 20);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
      ctx.font = "20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}`, canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 14);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#a5a5b8";
      ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText("Press R or click Restart", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 42);
    }
  }

  // === INPUT ==============================================================
  document.addEventListener("keydown", (ev) => {
    if (ev.key === "r" || ev.key === "R") { reset(); return; }
    keys.add(ev.key);
  });
  document.addEventListener("keyup", (ev) => { keys.delete(ev.key); });
  restartBtn.addEventListener("click", reset);

  // === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================
  function beep(freq, durSec) {
    try {
      if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
      const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
      const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
      osc.type = "square";
      osc.frequency.value = freq;
      gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.05, audioCtx.currentTime);
      gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
      osc.connect(gain); gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
      osc.start(); osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    } catch {}
  }

  // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ===============================================
  function publishState() {
    window.__game = {
      state: phase === "playing" ? "playing" : "gameover",
      score,
      player: {
        x: paddle.x,
        y: PADDLE_Y + PADDLE_H / 2,
        visible: paddle.x >= 0 && paddle.x <= canvas.width,
      },
      objective: "Bounce the ball with the paddle to break every brick.",
    };
  }

  // === HELPERS ============================================================
  function clamp(v, lo, hi) { return v < lo ? lo : (v > hi ? hi : v); }

  // === BOOT ===============================================================
  reset();
  </script>
</body>
</html>
```

The player can lose\n105: \n106: There must be a collision check (or other end condition) that fires `gameOver` and stops the active gameplay loop. \"Game runs forever, no death\" is broken. Examples that have failed in the past: collision check compared a value against itself; \"spike\" collision only clamped position instead of killing the player; obstacles never reached the player.\n107: \n108: ### 2. Implementation:

function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  function tick(now) {
    try {
       const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
       lastTime = now;
       // Clear canvas
       ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320"; // background color
       ctx.fillRect(0,0,canvas.width,canvas.height);
       // TODO: later update & render
    } catch(err) {
       console.error("loop error", err);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}

```js
    // === UPDATE ==============================================================
    function update(dt) {
        // Placeholder: increase score by 1 per second.
        // For now, increment based on dt.
        score += Math.floor(dt * 10); // 10 points per second
    }

    // === RENDER ==============================================================
    function render() {
        // Clear canvas
        ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
        ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

        // Draw player (simple circle)
        ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
        ctx.beginPath();
        ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, player.radius, 0, Math.PI*2);
        ctx.fill();

        // Draw score top-left
        ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
        ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
        ctx.textAlign = "left";
        ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
    }

    // === LOOP ==============================================================
    function startLoop() {
        if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
        function tick(now) {
            try {
                const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime)/1000, 0.1);
                lastTime = now;
                update(dt);
                render();
                // publishState(); // will be added later
            } catch(err) {
                console.error("loop error", err);
            }
            loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
        }
        loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }

    // Boot
    reset();
    startLoop();
```

      // Clear canvas
      ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

      // Draw hazards (if any)
      for (const h of hazards) {
        // Expect hazard object: {x, y, w, h, color, alive}
        if (h.alive === false) continue;
        ctx.fillStyle = h.color || "#ff5252"; // default color
        ctx.fillRect(h.x, h.y, h.w, h.h);

      ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
      ctx.beginPath();

      ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";

      ctx.textAlign = "left";

```
    function render() {
      // Clear canvas
      ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

      // Draw hazards (if any)
      for (const h of hazards) {
        // Expect hazard object: {x, y, w, h, color, alive}
        if (h.alive === false) continue;
        ctx.fillStyle = h.color || "#ff5252"; // default color
        ctx.fillRect(h.x, h.y, h.w, h.h);
      }

      // Draw player
      ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, player.radius, 0, Math.PI * 2);
      ctx.fill();

      // Draw HUD (score)
      ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
      ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.textAlign = "left";
      ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);

      // Game‑over overlay
      if (phase === "gameover") {
        ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(10,10,15,0.7)";
        ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
        ctx.textAlign = "center";
        ctx.font = "44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
        ctx.fillStyle = "#ff5252";
        ctx.fillText("Game Over", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 - 20);
        ctx.font = "20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
        ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
        ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}`, canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 14);
        ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
        ctx.fillStyle = "#a5a5b8";
        ctx.fillText("Press R or click Restart", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 42);
      }
    }
```

Make sure spacing is consistent.

Now apply edit.

prompt (5,105 chars)
Read CONVENTIONS.md and DESIGN.md in your working directory before doing anything else.

Your job in this stage is to break the implementation into a numbered list of small, ordered tasks. The next stage will work through this list one task at a time, in a single LLM session, reloading the page after each task and capturing console errors for you to fix.

Title: Quantum Leap
Concept: Tap to instantly warp the dot forward a set distance, leaping over upcoming obstacles. The warp has a short cooldown, requiring careful timing.

**You must produce one output file: `TASKS.md` in the working directory.** Use your write/edit tool to create this file on disk. After your turn ends, the validator runs `readFile("TASKS.md")` — if the file does not exist on disk, validation fails and your work is wasted. **Do NOT reply with the task list as inline chat text.** The list must be written to the file.

**Tool calls MUST be emitted as actual function/tool invocations — NOT described or simulated inside your reasoning/analysis text.** Writing tool-shaped JSON like `{"filePath":"TASKS.md","content":"..."}` *inside your chain-of-thought* does NOT invoke any tool. It is just text in the analysis channel that nothing reads or executes. The same goes for `{"pattern":"**/*"}` etc. — those only do something when emitted as a real `tool_use` event from the action channel, never when written into your thinking. Several prior runs have failed by hallucinating tool calls inside reasoning; do not repeat that pattern. If at any point you "describe" a tool call in your thinking instead of invoking it, you must immediately invoke the real tool afterward. The format MUST be a numbered list, one task per line, each line starting with `<number>.`:

```
1. <Task title> — <one-sentence description of what to do>
2. <Task title> — <one-sentence description>
...
```

The next stage parses tasks by matching `^\d+\.` at the start of each line. Don't use bullet sub-lists, don't number sub-tasks (1.1, 1.2). One number, one task, one line.

### What makes a good task

- **Small enough** to complete in a single coding turn (~50–150 lines of edits).
- **Testable**: after this task, what does the page do that it didn't before?
- **Leaves the file runnable**: at the end of the task, no syntax errors, no uncaught exceptions on load. Even if the game isn't playable yet, it must load.
- **Specific**: don't say "implement player". Say "implement player movement: arrow keys set keys.left/right; in update(), apply acceleration in the pressed direction with cap at MAX_SPEED, friction otherwise; clamp x to canvas bounds."
- **Concrete numbers**: this is where pixel sizes, ms intervals, and px/sec speeds live. Pick them. The implement stage will use them verbatim.

### Required coverage (in roughly this order)

Cover at minimum, ordered as below. Add tasks if the design demands more (powerups, level progression, screen wrap, etc):

1. **HTML scaffold + meta.json**: header (h1, description, controls list), canvas, restart button per CONVENTIONS.md. Stub `<script>` block (empty). meta.json matching header text.
2. **Game state objects**: player, hazards, score, phase using plain object literals at the top of the script. Set initial values.
3. **requestAnimationFrame loop**: startLoop() with `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` discipline at the top. Body wrapped in try/catch. Calls update(dt) and render(). Don't draw anything yet besides clearing the canvas.
4. **Input**: a `keys` Set populated by document keydown/keyup. R-key restart hook. Restart button click hook. Both call the same reset() function.
5. **Player movement**: dt-based physics tied to the key inputs from task 4. Verify each control listed in meta.json actually changes a value.
6. **Hazards**: data structure, spawn rules with a SAFE_RADIUS so nothing spawns on top of the player at t=0, motion (dt-scaled).
7. **Collision detection**: player vs hazards. On hit, transition phase to "gameover" and stop the active gameplay.
8. **Score**: discrete events that increase score, drawn on canvas at top-left.
9. **Render**: clear canvas, draw background, hazards, player, HUD. Player must be the highest-contrast object on screen.
10. **Game-over overlay**: dim background, "Game Over", final score, "Press R or click Restart".
11. **Observable contract**: populate `window.__game = { state, score, player:{x,y,visible}, objective }` every frame. `state` is "playing" | "gameover". `objective` is a 10–200 char single-sentence description.
12. **Audio**: lazy-init AudioContext inside a beep() helper, wrapped in try/catch. Hook into hit / score / death events.

You may merge two trivial tasks if they truly belong together (e.g. tasks 1+2 if state is just three lines). You may split a complex task in two. Aim for 10–15 tasks total.

**Final check before you stop**: confirm you actually called your write/edit tool with `TASKS.md` as the path. If you only described the task list in chat without invoking the tool, the file will not exist on disk and validation will fail. After the file is written, stop. Do not begin implementation.
